> DEVELOPER

Work on Linear issue ABA-375:

<issue identifier="ABA-375">
<title>N01: Establish the Information Architecture (IA)</title>
<description>
Before we begin the mechanical work of compressing prose, we need a finalized design document to standardize how we fold the 14 baseline skills into the new layout. This document prevents individual skills from drifting apart and ensures we don't accidentally drop critical gate criteria.

Objective is to create a design doc in `docs/designs/` that covers:

* **The Absorption Map:** A section-by-section breakdown of all 14 baseline skills, identifying what we keep, move, or delete.
* **The Survivor Manifest:** The final list of skills and utilities that will be used for the line-count audit.
* **A Standardized Naming Schema:** The fixed directory names and command wrappers for the new pack.
* **The Gate Inventory:** A clear destination for every [GATE] found in the original skills so that no requirements are lost.

**Operational Context**

* **What we are assuming:** We will use the naming schema from <issue id="6440b109-6e23-43b2-b176-38cc144bb14c" href="https://linear.app/ababushkin/issue/ABA-361/n04-execution-workflow-design-doc-before-build">ABA-361</issue> and limit the "measured set" to the shaping skills only, excluding Initiative A’s execution tools.
* **Risks we are managing:** To prevent the silent loss of gate criteria, we are making the **Gate Inventory** a mandatory part of the "Done" state. We must also prevent the document from creeping into actual prose drafting; it should stick strictly to maps, manifests, and naming rules.

**The Work**

1. **Draft the IA Document:** Create the initial absorption map and manifest. This task is complete once the document exists under `docs/designs/` with all 14 baseline skills mapped.
2. **Compile the Gate Inventory:** Audit the eight folded skills and document a destination for every [GATE]. This task is complete when every gate has a confirmed home or a recorded deletion rationale.

**Done When** This task is finished once the document is accepted, the naming is finalized, and we have a verified plan for every gate in the legacy library.
</description>

1	# Shaping pipeline — one path from idea to shipped work
2	
3	**Status:** Proposed (awaiting your confirmation on the four items marked _Proposed_)
4	**Date:** 2026-06-03
5	**Reverses:** the §17 decision (task-shape at pickup time), which lived on the now-closed PR #11 branch and never reached main
6	
7	---
8	
9	## What you asked for
10	
11	Three things, in your words:
12	
13	1. Shape lots of tickets quickly in a consistent format — from a pile of ideas or from a single idea, whether or not a goal exists yet.
14	2. Size the work so your agents never pick up a ticket that is too big.
15	3. Read the result in product language, sliced so you can review it easily.
16	
17	And one complaint underneath all three: the skills overlap, and the overlap is confusing.
18	
19	## The problem: two jobs wearing one coat
20	
21	The confusion has a single root. Every shaping skill today does the same job — break work into pieces — but each one writes for **agents** and demands a **rigid input**:
22	
23	- `delivery-shape` needs a committed goal with key results before it runs.
24	- `planning-and-task-breakdown` needs an accepted design document.
25	- `task-shape` needs a single ticket already sitting in the tracker.
26	
27	So when you arrive with a raw idea and want clean tickets you can review, none of them fit. You end up forcing product-shaped input through agent-shaped tools and finishing the job by hand.
28	
29	The fix is to separate two artefacts that have been mashed together:
30	
31	- **The thing you review** — tickets written in customer and business language, sliced so you can read down them and judge the work. Audience: you.
32	- **The thing your agents build** — an engineering breakdown into stacked pull requests, each tagged with the model that should run it. Audience: agents.
33	
34	One artefact, two audiences. Pull them apart and the overlap disappears.
35	
36	## The decision: split the work by who reads it
37	
38	```
39	YOU (product language)                      AGENTS (engineering)
40	──────────────────────                      ────────────────────
41	idea-triage       should we?  (optional)
42	initiative-shape  goal + KRs? (optional)
43	delivery-shape ──→ right-sized tickets ───→  execution-breakdown ──→ drain-cycle
44	  • single OR bulk ideas         (at pickup) • stacked PRs + model tiers   builds it
45	  • goal optional                            • runs right before the build
46	  • product language, easy to review
47	  • size-gated: no oversized tickets
48	```
49	
50	Two shapers, divided by who reads the output:
51	
52	- **`delivery-shape` becomes the shaper you use.** It takes a single idea or a whole pile, with or without a goal attached, and returns right-sized tickets in product language. It owns the size gate. This is the everyday workhorse.
53	- **`execution-breakdown` is the agent's tool** (renamed from `planning-and-task-breakdown`). It runs the moment an agent picks up a ticket, turns that ticket into a stack of pull requests, and tags each one with the model that should build it. You never drive it by hand.
54	
55	Two more moves clean up the rest:
56	
57	- **`task-shape` is retired** (pull request #11 is closed). "Shape one ticket" is simply `delivery-shape` with a single idea — it never needed its own skill.
58	- **The rename ends a name clash.** `planning-and-task-breakdown` shares its name with a skill in the upstream pack we derived it from. `execution-breakdown` is unmistakably ours, and the name says what it is: the breakdown that happens at execution time, for an agent, right before the build.
59	
60	## How you'll use it day to day
61	
62	You bring ideas. That is the only input that matters.
63	
64	- **A single idea, no goal in sight.** Run `delivery-shape` on it. You get one or more tickets, each a bounded chunk of customer value, sized to be picked up safely.
65	- **A backlog of ideas after a planning session.** Run `delivery-shape` over the set. You get a consistent ticket for each, grouped however the ideas cluster.
66	- **An idea that belongs to a goal.** Attach the goal and the tickets trace back to its key results. Skip the goal and the tickets simply stand on their own.
67	
68	In every case the output reads the same way and slices the same way, so review feels familiar no matter where the idea came from.
69	
70	## What a ticket looks like _(Decided: keep the five-section body, softened to product language)_
71	
72	Each ticket keeps `delivery-shape`'s existing five-section body — **What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks** — so the format stays consistent with every plan already shaped and the enforcement gate (`bin/check-plan-framing`) is untouched. What changes is the *voice*: the sections are written in customer and business language, not engineering language.
73	
74	- **What** — the outcome: who gets what value, in plain language. ("A returning customer sees their saved addresses at checkout.")
75	- **Why** — the bet this ticket makes and what it unlocks.
76	- **Completion** — the finish line in business terms, not code terms.
77	- **Assumptions / Key Risks** — what we're taking on faith and what could go wrong, stated so a product reader follows them.
78	
79	The fine-grained engineering detail — the order of pull requests, the per-slice model tier — is *not* in the ticket; it's produced by the agent's `execution-breakdown` at pickup. Your ticket stays a product artefact.
80	
81	## The size gate: nothing too big reaches an agent _(Proposed)_
82	
83	A ticket is too big when either test fails:
84	
85	- It would break into **more than three to five slices**, or
86	- It covers **more than one customer outcome**.
87	
88	When a ticket fails, `delivery-shape` splits it into sibling tickets before you ever see it as one lump. The measure is slice count, never hours or days — size is about how much there is to verify, not how long it takes. This gate is the guarantee you asked for: your agents pick up bounded work every time.
89	
90	## When there's no goal _(Proposed)_
91	
92	The goal link is optional, and the ticket format does not change when you drop it:
93	
94	- **With a goal** — tickets group under the outcomes they serve, and each traces back to a key result.
95	- **Without a goal** — tickets stand alone, with no grouping layer and no key-result trace.
96	
97	A pile of ideas becomes a set of ticket groups in one pass. The shaper never invents a goal to justify the structure; it shapes only what you bring it.
98	
99	## The handoff to your agents _(Proposed)_
100	
101	Each shaped ticket carries two things across the line to your agents:
102	
103	- A **rough size signal** (the expected slice count) so you can read capacity at a glance before any build starts.
104	- A standing instruction: **when picked up, expand through `execution-breakdown`.**
105	
106	An agent picks up the ticket, runs `execution-breakdown` to produce the stacked pull requests and their model tiers, and builds. The fine-grained breakdown happens on fresh context at the last responsible moment — so it reflects what is true at build time, not what was guessed weeks earlier.
107	
108	## What changes in the pack
109	
110	| Artefact | Change |
111	|---|---|
112	| `delivery-shape` | Accept single or bulk ideas; make the goal optional; rewrite output to product language; add the size gate. |
113	| `planning-and-task-breakdown` → `execution-breakdown` | Rename the directory, the command, and the frontmatter name. Reposition as the agent's just-in-time breakdown. |
114	| `task-shape` (PR #11) | Closed. Folded into `delivery-shape`'s single-idea mode. |
115	| `docs/design-decisions.md` | Record this decision; supersede §17. |
116	| References to the old name | Update `delivery-shape`, `references/task-sizing.md`, `design-doc`, `README.md`, `using-this-pack`, `install.sh`. |
117	
118	## Open questions to confirm
119	
120	The four sections marked _Proposed_ above are my best defaults, not settled calls:
121	
122	1. **Ticket format** — ~~resolved~~: keep the five-section body (What / Why / Completion / Assumptions / Key Risks), softened to product language. No gate change.
123	2. **Size gate** — is "three to five slices, or one outcome" the right line?
124	3. **No-goal mode** — flat standalone tickets when no goal exists: right, or do you want a lighter grouping?
125	4. **Handoff** — does the slice-count signal give you the capacity view you want, or do you need a cost or model-mix estimate up front too?
126	
127	One question sits outside this doc: **`drain-cycle`** is not defined in this pack. Its contract — what it reads from a ticket and how it runs the build — decides whether the handoff above is complete. Confirm whether `drain-cycle` lives in the verify-flow project, or whether we define it here.
128	
129	## Decision record (supersedes §17)
130	
131	§17 placed ticket shaping at **pickup time**, inside the agent session, as `task-shape`. This doc reverses that. Shaping you can review happens at **shape time**, in product language, in `delivery-shape`. The engineering breakdown happens at pickup, in `execution-breakdown`, for the agent. The two jobs were never the same job, and §17's mistake was treating them as one.
132	
```

1	---
2	name: execution-workflow
3	status: accepted
4	authors: Anton Babushkin
5	created: 2026-06-10
6	last_updated: 2026-06-10
7	supersedes: none
8	namespace_record: this doc + ADR 0004 are the record of the `exec:*` namespace decision; downstream skills bind to ADR 0004.
9	---
10	
11	# Execution workflow — verb namespace, skill graph, handoff contract
12	
13	## Problem
14	
15	A drain-cycle worker picks up a Linear issue and must reach a merged PR with the full review trail. Today the worker improvises: there is no single front-door skill, no agreed verb namespace for the execution-side skills, and no contract for what each step receives from the previous one or hands to the next. The acceptance criteria written on the issue do not reliably reach the review step — reviewers grade code quality but cannot grade *built the wrong thing* because the AC never travelled. Workers running on non-Claude surfaces (codex, kimi) have no tool to fan out reviews and silently fall back to no review at all.
16	
17	Affected parties: drain-cycle workers (every drained issue), reviewers (the persona dispatch in N03 + the human reading PRs), the supervisor prompt in initiative C (a single pointer is impossible until the front-door skill exists), the initiative B consolidation (cannot consolidate verbs it does not own).
18	
19	Current behaviour: ad hoc steps, ad hoc names, AC drops between issue and review, no fan-out outside Claude Code. Desired behaviour: a named verb namespace, a graph of named delegations, and a single handoff contract that carries the issue's AC end-to-end on every worker surface this pack targets.
20	
21	## Context
22	
23	D1 has landed: ADR 0003 pins the persona contract (file format + `file:line · class · severity` finding triples + the Claude-Code-Agent / inline-sequential dispatch branches); N03's `execution-review` skill consumes that contract. N01 ships the fixture corpus + grader. D2's bet (KR1 — ≥4/5 drained issues reach Done with a merged PR and full review trail) lives in N05–N08, all blocked by this design doc.
24	
25	The verb namespace is a one-way door. `drain_cycle/prompt.py` and any sibling supervisor prompt in initiative C will name these verbs literally; once workers' muscle memory and external prompts bind, a rename costs a coordinated migration across every drainer. Shaping verbs (`shape:*`) are already established — this doc names execution verbs only, but reserves both halves of the namespace in a single table so initiative B's consolidation has one source of truth.
26	
27	**Supervisor-binding audit (performed before pinning, per the N04 to-verify assumption).** A read of current `drain_cycle/prompt.py` (and a grep of the rest of the drain-cycle source tree) shows the supervisor today emits exactly two execution-side verbs literally:
28	
29	- `/shape:task` — once, only on `verify`-labelled issues, via the shape-task directive (check-first against an existing Task-Shaper block in the issue body).
30	- `/code-review-and-quality` — hard-coded in four string locations (`_TAIL`, `_STACK_TAIL`, and the numbered completion sequence in both the normal and stack preambles).
31	
32	The rest of the completion sequence (commit/push → review-summary comment → Linear Done transition) is **inlined as prose in the preamble, not delegated to named verbs.** The `/shape:verify-implementation`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, and `/shape:pr-respond` verbs that an earlier draft of this doc assumed were live appear only in drain-cycle's `.entire/` git history — they are *not* in current source. Migration consequence: adopting `exec:*` is a **bounded string swap plus an additive replacement**, not a coordinated cross-drainer migration — see Open question Q1 (resolved) for the exact cutover.
33	
34	Predecessor packs are tracker-blind: `addyosmani/agent-skills` (`/spec`, `/plan`, `/build`, `/test`, `/review`, `/ship`) and `obra/superpowers` (`subagent-driven-development`, `executing-plans`, `finishing-a-development-branch`) converge on the same shape — pickup → breakdown → red/green loop → review → finish — but neither carries issue acceptance criteria through the handoff and neither dispatches a persona fan-out.
35	
36	## Constraints
37	
38	**Functional.**
39	
40	- Every D2 skill is reachable by *name* from the front-door skill — no inlined procedure (Rule P7). This includes `breakdown-at-pickup`, which is a **named delegation** invoked by `exec:pickup`, not an inlined sub-procedure (see the skill graph).
41	- The issue's acceptance criteria are carried by the handoff contract from pickup to the review step and surface verbatim as input to the spec-compliance persona.
42	- Persona dispatch follows ADR 0003: Claude-Code Agent fan-out by default, inline-sequential fallback on non-Claude workers; the supervisor — not the persona — chooses the branch.
43	- The PR-finishing step produces the trail artefacts KR1 greps for: What/Why/Focus PR body + review-summary comment + Linear status transition.
44	
45	**Non-functional.**
46	
47	| NFR | Target | Fitness function |
48	|---|---|---|
49	| Headless-first | The front door, build, debug, simplify, review-fan-out, verify, and PR-finishing skills run end-to-end inside one non-interactive `claude -p` invocation against a seeded issue with no human prompt between steps. | The N09 validation-drain harness runs 5 issues headlessly; any step that re-prompts a human fails the drain and the skill that re-prompted is named in the failure. |
50	| Vendor portability | Every execution verb resolves to one skill file authored against this pack's anatomy; no skill depends on a Claude-Code-only tool except via the ADR-0003 dispatch branch (which has a documented inline-sequential fallback). | A grep over the D2 skills' workflow sections finds zero references to vendor-specific tool names outside the dispatch branch's `# Claude Code` / `# Other workers` split. |
51	| Front-door size | The front-door skill stays ≤ 200 lines so the named-delegation discipline is structurally enforced (inlining is structurally impossible at that size). | `wc -l skills/<front-door>/SKILL.md` ≤ 200 at acceptance. |
52	| Verb collision | Zero collisions between shaping (`shape:*`) and execution verbs at the slash-command surface. | A diff over `.claude/commands/` after authoring shows no two commands sharing a leaf name. |
53	| AC carry-through | The spec-compliance persona's input includes the verbatim issue AC checklist on every drained issue. | An N09 drain inspects the review-step prompt log — absence of the verbatim AC list is a drain failure attributed to the handoff contract, not the persona.|
54	
55	## Alternatives considered
56	
57	**Alt 1 — Bare verbs (`/pickup`, `/build`, `/review`, `/finish`).**
58	
59	*Description.* No namespace prefix on execution verbs; only shaping keeps `shape:*`.
60	*Blast radius if wrong.* Hostile to slash-command discovery (autocomplete shows shaping and execution interleaved), and `/review` collides with several installed packs the owner already uses (`code-review`, `crit`, the built-in `review` command). Rename later means rewriting `drain_cycle/prompt.py`, every initiative-C supervisor prompt, and any reference in installed plugins.
61	*Reversal cost.* High — once the supervisor in initiative C binds, every downstream worker has to be re-keyed.
62	
63	**Alt 2 — `exec:*` prefix on every execution verb (`exec:pickup`, `exec:build`, `exec:review`, `exec:verify`, `exec:finish`).**
64	
65	*Description.* Mirrors the established `shape:*` discipline on the execution half. Two clean halves of one namespace.
66	*Blast radius if wrong.* Low. Verbose at the slash-command surface but unambiguous; autocomplete groups every execution verb. Recovers if a single verb name turns out wrong by renaming inside one prefix without touching the rest.
67	*Reversal cost.* Low per verb, because the prefix anchors the others.
68	
69	**Alt 3 — `drain:*` prefix (`drain:pickup`, `drain:build`, …).**
70	
71	*Description.* Names the workflow that owns the verbs.
72	*Blast radius if wrong.* Couples the verb namespace to one supervisor (`drain-cycle`). Initiative C's premise is that the workflow lives in the *pack*, not in the supervisor; binding the verb to the supervisor's name re-creates the very coupling the initiative exists to remove.
73	*Reversal cost.* High — same problem as Alt 1 plus a semantic miscue.
74	
75	**Alt 4 (do nothing) — leave the execution skills nameless.**
76	
77	*Description.* Each D2 skill chooses its own leaf name; no shared prefix.
78	*Blast radius if wrong.* The front door and the supervisor prompt both end up referencing each skill by an ad-hoc string. No collision detection. No grouping at the slash-command surface. The initiative-B consolidation has no namespace to consolidate to.
79	*Reversal cost.* High — picking the namespace later means renaming after binding.
80	
81	## Recommended approach
82	
83	**Adopt Alt 2: `exec:*` prefix on every execution verb.** Recorded as the decision of record in **ADR 0004**. Namespace table reserved here:
84	
85	| Half | Prefix | Verbs (this pack) |
86	|---|---|---|
87	| Shaping | `shape:` | `shape:idea-triage`, `shape:initiative`, `shape:roadmap`, `shape:delivery`, `shape:design-doc`, `shape:plan-review`, … (existing) |
88	| Execution | `exec:` | `exec:pickup` (front door), `exec:build`, `exec:debug`, `exec:simplify`, `exec:review` (persona fan-out), `exec:verify`, `exec:finish` |
89	
90	`exec:` wins on the named constraint (vendor portability + verb-collision). It mirrors `shape:` so the worker reads one consistent rule across the whole pack. It is the only candidate that survives initiative C's portability premise (Alt 3 fails), keeps clean slash-command discovery (Alt 1 fails), and avoids a deferred naming bill (Alt 4 fails).
91	
92	**Skill graph (named delegations only, no inlined procedure).**
93	
94	```
95	            [issue picked up]
96	                  │
97	                  ▼
98	            exec:pickup        ─── front door (N05)
99	                  │
100	                  ▼
101	    exec:breakdown             ─── named delegation invoked by exec:pickup;
102	                  │                emits the ordered task list (NOT inlined)
103	        ┌─────────┴──────────┐
104	        ▼                    │
105	    exec:build (N06)         │  per slice
106	        │  RED → GREEN → commit
107	        │
108	        ├─ red-loop stuck ──▶ exec:debug (N07)  ──▶ back to RED
109	        │
110	        ├─ green ───────────▶ exec:simplify (N07) ─▶ commit
111	        │
112	        └─ slice done ───────▶ next slice ↑ or ↓
113	                  │
114	                  ▼
115	           exec:review        ─── N03's execution-review (already shipped)
116	                  │            fans out personas per ADR 0003
117	                  ▼
118	            exec:verify       ─── checks the AC checklist against the diff
119	                  │
120	                  ▼
121	            exec:finish (N08) ─── Graphite-first, git fallback, trail artefacts
122	                  │
123	                  ▼
124	        [PR open + status moved to In Review/Done]
125	```
126	
127	Each arrow is a named delegation in the front-door skill's workflow section. `exec:breakdown` is the published verb form of breakdown-at-pickup — it satisfies Rule P7 (the front door names it, does not inline it) and keeps `exec:pickup` under the 200-line NFR.
128	
129	**Inter-skill handoff contract (the envelope each step receives + emits).**
130	
131	| Step | Receives | Emits |
132	|---|---|---|
133	| `exec:pickup` | issue id + branch + worktree path | `pickup-envelope.json`: `{issue_id, branch, ac_checklist[], body_md, labels[], blocked_by[]}` and an ordered task list (each task: `id`, `done_when`, `model_tier`, `axes`, `risk`) |
134	| `exec:build` (per slice) | one task + `pickup-envelope.json` | a commit per slice + an updated `build-log.md` recording RED/GREEN gates |
135	| `exec:debug` | the failing-check output + the current envelope | a written root-cause note (gate) + handed control back to `exec:build` |
136	| `exec:simplify` | the green diff + envelope | a before/after rationale appended to `build-log.md` |
137	| `exec:review` *(provisional — see Q2)* | working-tree diff + `pickup-envelope.json` (so personas see `ac_checklist`) | the deduped GO/NO-GO verdict + finding triples per ADR 0003 |
138	| `exec:verify` *(provisional — see Q2)* | `ac_checklist` + diff | a structured pass/fail per AC item; any fail loops back into `exec:build` |
139	| `exec:finish` | envelope + review verdict + verify result | PR body (What/Why/Focus), review-summary comment, Linear status move |
140	
141	The envelope is the carrier. The `ac_checklist` field is the answer to the original failure mode: the spec-compliance persona consumes it directly, so *built-the-wrong-thing* is reviewable. The two rows marked *provisional* are settled at N05 authoring if `exec:verify` and `exec:review` collapse (Q2); if they collapse, those two rows merge into one.
142	
143	**Persona-dispatch integration.** `exec:review` is N03's already-shipped `execution-review` skill, renamed to live under the `exec:` prefix. **The rename is owned at N05 authoring time** (N05 publishes the front door and the verb namespace together); N04 reserves the name, N05 performs the rename. ADR 0003's dispatch branches are the skill's responsibility, not the front door's — the front door delegates by name and is dispatch-agnostic.
144	
145	## Consequences
146	
147	**Positive.**
148	
149	- One naming table covers both halves; initiative B's consolidation consumes it.
150	- The envelope's `ac_checklist` field closes the AC-carry-through gap on every drained issue.
151	- The front-door skill stays ≤ 200 lines because every step is a one-line delegation.
152	- Vendor portability holds: only `exec:review` knows about ADR 0003's branches; the rest of the graph is dispatch-agnostic.
153	- N05–N08 can break their tasks against a stable contract without further negotiation.
154	
155	**Negative.**
156	
157	- `exec:` is verbose at the slash-command surface compared to bare verbs.
158	- The envelope is a new artefact workers must write; envelope drift (a field renamed in one skill, not the others) becomes a real failure mode and must be policed by `exec:pickup`'s skeleton task.
159	- N09's validation drain becomes the only place envelope-field drift surfaces; an envelope schema lint should follow if drift recurs.
160	
161	**Walking skeleton (Rule B2).** Required. The skeleton is one issue drained end-to-end with the simplest possible body in each step — `exec:build` doing a one-line edit, `exec:review` running with one persona, `exec:finish` opening a draft PR. The N05 skeleton task ("a dry run on one real issue reaches the PR step") is the walking skeleton for the whole graph. It must land before N06–N08's substantive content is filled in. Pre-skeleton estimates of envelope correctness are uncalibrated.
162	
163	## Operability plan
164	
165	- **Metrics.** Per drained issue: time-to-PR, AC-pass-rate at `exec:verify`, persona finding count by class/severity, count of `exec:debug` escalations, count of post-push manual fix commits.
166	- **Structured logs.** Each skill emits one JSON line per step: `{skill, issue_id, step, gate_result, duration_ms, envelope_hash}`. The hash detects envelope drift between steps.
167	- **Traces.** Spans per skill invocation parented to the pickup span; an attribute `envelope.ac_count` makes AC-carry-through queryable.
168	- **Alerts.** None at this stage — drain volume is too low for thresholds. The N09 validation drain is the alert surface.
169	- **Rollback plan.** If a verb name turns out wrong: (1) author the new name as a sibling skill, (2) leave the old name as a one-line redirect for one cycle, (3) update `drain_cycle/prompt.py` and initiative-C supervisor prompts, (4) delete the redirect. Verification gate at each step: run one validation drain and confirm zero re-prompts.
170	- **Capacity headroom.** The envelope is small (≤ 2 KB per drain). No capacity concern in this pack.
171	- **Known failure modes.** (a) Envelope field drift between skills → policed by envelope hash in logs + N09 drift check. (b) Persona dispatch branch mis-selected on a non-Claude worker → ADR 0003's persona prose is identical across branches; failure surfaces as zero findings, caught by the N01 grader on the next validation drain. (c) `exec:debug` infinite loop → bound by a "consecutive-failure escalation" gate inside `exec:build` (per N06).
172	- **Upstream dependencies.** Linear API (read AC + write status), GitHub/Graphite (open PR). Failure mode: Linear API down → `exec:pickup` halts with a comment; `exec:finish` halts and queues the PR move locally.
173	- **Downstream dependencies.** `drain_cycle/prompt.py` (initiative C) consumes only the front-door verb; the rest of the graph is the front door's concern.
174	
175	## Open questions
176	
177	| Q | Owner | Resolution gate |
178	|---|---|---|
179	| **Q1 (RESOLVED by the supervisor-binding audit above).** Does `drain_cycle/prompt.py`'s current flow need to migrate to `exec:*`, or can it be adapted in-place? | initiative-C owner | **Resolved.** The audit found two live bindings: `/code-review-and-quality` (4 string locations) → swap to `exec:review`; `/shape:task` (verify-flow directive) → folds into `exec:pickup`'s breakdown. The inlined completion-sequence prose is where `exec:verify`/`exec:finish` land *additively*. Cutover is a bounded edit to `prompt.py`, scheduled at the initiative-C pointer-swap (N10); no coordinated cross-drainer migration. The earlier-assumed `/shape:verify-implementation`/`/shape:pr-prepare`/`/shape:pr-respond` verbs are not in current source. |
180	| **Q2.** Should `exec:verify` and `exec:review` collapse into one skill (both consume AC)? The two *provisional* rows in the handoff-contract table depend on this. | N05 skeleton task | Resolved when the front-door skill is first authored. If the two collapse cleanly without inlining a procedure, do so and merge the two table rows; otherwise keep separate. |
181	| **Q3.** Does the envelope need a versioned schema field from day one, or can drift detection wait for N09 to find a real case? | N05 skeleton task | Resolved at N05 authoring. Default: no version field; add only if N09 surfaces drift in the first 5 drains. |
182	| **Q4.** Are there worker surfaces beyond Claude Code, codex, and kimi that need explicit treatment in the dispatch branch? | initiative-C owner | Resolved before initiative C cutover; not blocking D2. |
183	
184	## Review record
185	
186	Accepted through the `plan-review` exit gate (REVISE → conditions cleared). Review record: `docs/plan-reviews/execution-workflow/review.md`. Blocking conditions 1 (supervisor-binding audit) and 2 (ADR of record) closed by the Context audit + ADR 0004; clarity conditions 3–4 folded into the skill graph, the handoff table, and the persona-dispatch note above.
187	
```

```
1	# ADR 0004 — Execution verb namespace (`exec:*`)
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-06-10
5	- **Serves:** Initiative D2 / KR1 — drained issues reach Done with a merged PR and full review trail (N04–N08).
6	- **Premise for:** Initiative B (verb consolidation consumes this namespace table) and Initiative C (the drain-cycle supervisor binds the front-door verb literally).
7	- **Record of:** the namespace decision in `docs/design-docs/execution-workflow/design-doc.md` (N04). The design doc carries the full alternatives analysis and skill graph; this ADR is the durable decision downstream skills bind to.
8	
9	## Context
10	
11	D2 authors five execution-side skills (front door, build, debug/simplify, review fan-out, verify, PR finishing). Each must be reachable by name from the front-door skill, and the drain-cycle supervisor in initiative C will name the front-door verb literally in `drain_cycle/prompt.py`. The verb namespace is a one-way door: once the supervisor prompt and workers' muscle memory bind, a rename costs a coordinated migration across every drainer.
12	
13	Shaping verbs (`shape:*`) are already established. The execution half was unnamed. Four candidates were weighed in the N04 design doc:
14	
15	1. **Bare verbs** (`/pickup`, `/build`, `/review`) — rejected: `/review` collides with installed packs (`code-review`, `crit`, built-in `review`); no slash-command grouping.
16	2. **`exec:*` prefix** — mirrors `shape:*`; two clean halves of one namespace; per-verb rename stays local to the prefix.
17	3. **`drain:*` prefix** — rejected: couples the namespace to one supervisor, re-creating the pack-vs-supervisor coupling initiative C exists to remove.
18	4. **Nameless** — rejected: no collision detection, nothing for initiative B to consolidate to.
19	
20	**Supervisor-binding audit (performed before pinning).** A read of current `drain_cycle/prompt.py` confirmed the migration surface is bounded: the supervisor emits `/code-review-and-quality` (4 string locations) and `/shape:task` (verify-flow directive only); the rest of the completion sequence is inlined prose. Adopting `exec:*` is a bounded string swap plus an additive replacement, not a coordinated cross-drainer migration. The audit also corrected a stale assumption — `/shape:verify-implementation`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, and `/shape:pr-respond` are not in current source.
21	
22	## Decision
23	
24	**Adopt the `exec:*` prefix for every execution-side verb.** Reserved namespace table (both halves, single source of truth for initiative B):
25	
26	| Half | Prefix | Verbs (this pack) |
27	|---|---|---|
28	| Shaping | `shape:` | `shape:idea-triage`, `shape:initiative`, `shape:roadmap`, `shape:delivery`, `shape:design-doc`, `shape:plan-review`, … (existing) |
29	| Execution | `exec:` | `exec:pickup` (front door), `exec:breakdown`, `exec:build`, `exec:debug`, `exec:simplify`, `exec:review`, `exec:verify`, `exec:finish` |
30	
31	`exec:review` is N03's already-shipped `execution-review` skill, renamed under the prefix at N05 authoring time.
32	
33	## Consequences
34	
35	**Positive**
36	- Mirrors `shape:*`; one consistent namespacing rule across the whole pack.
37	- Per-verb renames stay local to the prefix — low reversal cost for a single verb.
38	- Initiative B consolidates against one reserved table; initiative C binds one front-door verb.
39	
40	**Negative / costs**
41	- Verbose at the slash-command surface versus bare verbs. Accepted — discovery grouping and collision-avoidance outweigh verbosity.
42	- The drain-cycle cutover requires a bounded edit to `prompt.py` (the N10 pointer-swap). Scheduled, not free.
43	
44	## Scope
45	
46	This ADR pins the prefix and reserves the leaf names. The skill graph, handoff contract, and per-skill behaviour live in the N04 design doc; the skills themselves are authored in N05–N08. Any change to the prefix or a reserved leaf name requires a follow-up ADR.
47	
```

{"id":"962be170-d2f2-4b4e-99e5-19f01140c3b4","icon":null,"color":"#bec2c8","name":"Consolidate Shaper’s Lifecycle into Four Phase Skills","summary":"Collapse the shaping half of Shaper into four phase skills (shape:idea/project/design/delivery), each under the length cap, every pruned skill absorbed or deleted. Second of three lifecycle-expansion projects.","description":"The current Shaper library is spread across 11 skills, totaling 2,357 lines. Because several components exceed our 300-line limit and the fragmented structure slows down both humans and agents, we will consolidate the shaping process into four core phase skills: `shape:idea`, `shape:project`, `shape:design`, and `shape:delivery`.\n\nOur goal is to ensure every new skill is gate-complete and fits within our technical constraints without losing the logic of the original content. We will measure our progress against these targets:\n\n* **Footprint:** Reduce the total shaping library to ≤2,200 lines, ensuring no single skill exceeds 300 lines.\n* **Performance:** Maintain the `plan-review` benchmark pass rate at ≥90% to prove that tightening the prose does not harm the model's accuracy.\n* **Cleanup:** Remove all legacy command wrappers and citations from the `README`, `install.sh`, and internal hooks.\n\nIf we cannot fit a phase skill under 350 lines while keeping its required gates, or if the benchmark fails to stay above 80% after two attempts, we will stop and ship style reforms only.","url":"https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/consolidate-shapers-lifecycle-into-four-phase-skills-9c8a65552b93","createdAt":"2026-06-10T03:09:46.260Z","updatedAt":"2026-06-11T23:49:13.276Z","startedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"canceledAt":null,"startDate":null,"startDateResolution":null,"targetDate":null,"targetDateResolution":null,"priority":{"value":0,"name":"No priority"},"labels":[],"initiatives":[],"lead":{"id":"720d0535-dfc6-465f-8111-d1a3e6eab786","name":"Anton Babushkin"},"status":{"id":"397ab5c0-7cdf-47ad-b862-02acfd85c92f","name":"Planned","type":"planned"},"teams":[{"id":"c20f56da-f121-4653-bf6c-dc1c8aac21c1","name":"Personal","key":"ABA"}]}

1	# Lifecycle expansion — shaping → execution
2	
3	Status: **confirmed and parked** — all three initiatives recorded in Linear (Personal team, Planned state) on 2026-06-10:
4	[A — Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/issues-drain-end-to-end-on-first-party-skills-be562671c8d8) ·
5	[B — Shaping consolidates to four dense front doors](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/shaping-consolidates-to-four-dense-front-doors-9c8a65552b93) ·
6	[C — drain-cycle supervises; the pack owns the workflow](https://linear.app/ababushkin/project/drain-cycle-supervises-the-pack-owns-the-workflow-0f0222d99392)
7	Date: 2026-06-10
8	Inputs: deep review of obra/superpowers (5.1.0) and addyosmani/agent-skills (2e0dfbfb436e) installed plugins, audit of this repo, audit of ~/src/drain-cycle; interview-me session with confirmed intent.
9	
10	---
11	
12	## 1. Deep-review findings (condensed)
13	
14	### Shaper today
15	
16	14 skills (~3,086 lines), 2 hooks, 1 sub-agent (code-reviewer), 10 references, 3 rule files. Coverage is front-heavy by design: Define/Plan/Decide deep; **Build 0%, Deploy 0%, Ship partial** (pr-prepare, verify-implementation, code-reviewer). Three skills bust the pack's own 300-line cap: plan-review (369), initiative-shape (310), roadmap-shape (303).
17	
18	The "too lengthy" instinct, measured: median skill is ~225 lines — *between* superpowers (~190) and agent-skills (~290). What differs is density, not length: superpowers makes every section a gate, dot-graph, rationalization table, or prompt template, with 2 frontmatter fields vs Shaper's 10+ and no 1,700-line rules/references sidecar.
19	
20	### What the other packs have that Shaper lacks
21	
22	- **superpowers execution engine** — brainstorming → writing-plans (2–5 min TDD steps, exact code) → subagent-driven-development (fresh implementer subagent per task; two-stage review: *spec compliance before code quality*; never trust the implementer's report; built-in cheap/standard/frontier model routing) → finishing-a-development-branch. Worktree isolation as prerequisite. Three-layer verification: plan-step commands → per-task dual review → claim-level fresh-evidence gate (verification-before-completion).
23	- **agent-skills delivery loop** — /spec → /plan → /build (per-task RED/GREEN/commit) → /ship (parallel fan-out to code-reviewer + security-auditor + test-engineer personas → GO/NO-GO with mandatory rollback plan). Personas are ~100-line portable role prompts with a composition doctrine: skills = how, personas = who, commands = when; personas never call personas.
24	
25	### What neither pack has (Shaper's edge)
26	
27	Both are **tracker-blind and PR-blind**: no Linear, no Graphite, no stacked PRs, no auto-merge policy, no autonomous runner. Shaper + drain-cycle already own that territory — drain-cycle spawns workers per Linear issue in worktrees, routes models via labels, and its verify-flow already expects `/shape:task`, `/shape:verify-implementation`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, `/shape:pr-respond`, with a half-built Graphite stack mode (`.drain-handoff.json`).
28	
29	**Conflict to resolve:** `drain_cycle/prompt.py` hardcodes `/code-review-and-quality` (an agent-skills skill). Uninstalling that pack breaks drains unless Shaper owns a replacement.
30	
31	---
32	
33	## 2. Confirmed intent (interview-me output)
34	
35	- **Outcome:** Shaper becomes the single pack covering the full lifecycle — `shape:*` for planning (4 front doors: `shape:idea`, `shape:project`, `shape:design`, `shape:delivery`) and a second verb namespace (e.g. `ship:*`, verb TBD) for execution: breakdown-at-pickup → TDD/incremental build → systematic debugging → simplification → reviewer fan-out → verify → Graphite-first PR finishing. superpowers and agent-skills get uninstalled.
36	- **User:** Headless drain-cycle workers first; interactive second. drain-cycle re-scoped to a thin vendor-agnostic supervisor (spawn, guardrails, halt, grade) whose prompt points at one skill — the workflow lives in the pack so a codex/kimi worker can follow the same prose.
37	- **Why now:** Planning half is deep but execution is 0%; drain-cycle's verify-flow half-expects skills that don't exist; two external packs fill the gap.
38	- **Success:** Both external packs uninstalled with nothing missed in practice; workers complete issues end-to-end on pack skills only; artefacts optimized for correctness and review-ease.
39	- **Constraints:** Linear for state; Graphite-first PRs (plain git fallback); sub-agents as portable persona prompt files (code-reviewer, security-auditor, spec-compliance) — Agent-tool dispatch on Claude Code, inline fallback elsewhere. Style reform: superpowers density, frontmatter cut to essentials, ~150-line target for execution skills, 3 over-cap skills brought under 300.
40	- **Pruning:** roadmap-shape, backlog-manage, app-calibrate deleted; idea-triage folds into `shape:idea`; spikes fold into `shape:design`; plan-review survives as the design/delivery exit gate; render-html survives as a utility.
41	- **Domain shortlist (in):** systematic debugging, code simplification, security-review persona. **(Out):** frontend/perf/browser set.
42	- **Out of scope this round:** deployment stage, implementer sub-agents (drain-cycle's job), cross-vendor adversarial review (future drain-cycle feature), renames (pack umbrella + drain-cycle).
43	
44	### Architecture decision: drain-cycle re-scope
45	
46	drain-cycle owns *processes* (cycle selection, worktree setup, vendor-agnostic worker spawning, token/time/cost guardrails, halt/resume, run logs/grading); Shaper owns *workflow* (the intra-issue procedure and reviewer personas). drain-cycle's inlined prompt procedure shrinks to "work issue X in worktree Y; follow the entry skill." Personas live as portable prompt files so non-Claude workers degrade to inline self-review with the same persona prompt. Cross-vendor adversarial review (Claude implements, codex reviews) becomes a later drain-cycle feature.
47	
48	---
49	
50	## 3. Split — three initiatives
51	
52	Total scope exceeds the 15-issue split threshold. Cut:
53	
54	| # | Initiative | Scope | Order |
55	|---|---|---|---|
56	| A | **Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills** | Execution namespace skills (~5–6), 2–3 personas, fixture harness + grader, one-line drain-cycle prompt pointer, validation drains | first — shaped below |
57	| B | **Shaping pack consolidation + style reform** | 4 front doors, prune (roadmap/backlog/calibrate), fold triage→idea and spikes→design, density reform, frontmatter cut, 3 over-cap skills under 300 | second — to be shaped |
58	| C | **drain-cycle re-scope to thin supervisor** | Extract workflow from prompt.py, guardrail/grade cleanup, Graphite stack-mode completion, cross-vendor worker spawning | third — to be shaped |
59	
60	---
61	
62	## 4. Initiative A — draft shape (awaiting confirmation)
63	
64	**Name:** Issues drain end-to-end on first-party skills
65	
66	**Goal:** For drain-cycle workers (and Anton driving interactively), make every picked-up Linear issue flow from pickup to a merged, reviewable PR on Shaper skills alone — review trail attached, no rescue by a human and no reliance on third-party packs.
67	
68	**Key results:**
69	
70	**KR1 (stretch)** — ≥4 of the next 5 drained issues reach Done with a merged PR and zero manual fix commits after the worker's final push, with the full review trail present (verify verdict, What/Why/Focus PR body, review-summary comment) *(bet)*
71	- baseline: 0 — execution skills don't exist; current drains depend on `/code-review-and-quality` and push unreviewed to main
72	- target: ≥4 of 5
73	- measured over: the first 5 issues drained after the execution skills land (within 2 cycles)
74	- how we'll know: `drain-cycle grade` over `~/.drain-cycle/runs/*.json` + `git log` scan for post-push fix commits; review trail checked from Linear issue comments and PR bodies
75	
76	*Layer 1: outcome · Layer 2: self-trial protocol*
77	
78	**KR2 (commit)** — the review/verify skills surface ≥9 of 10 seeded Critical/Required findings when run against fixture diffs with known defects (type suppressions, AC violations, security holes) *(foundation)*
79	- baseline: 0/10 — fixtures don't exist; building the fixture set + grader is the first issue
80	- target: ≥9/10 seeded findings surfaced
81	- measured over: by cycle close
82	- how we'll know: `bin/grade-execution-review fixtures/execution-review/` runs each persona against each fixture diff and diffs surfaced findings against the seeded manifest; exit code + log cached in `fixtures/execution-review/_runs/`
83	
84	*Layer 1: correctness · Layer 2: golden-path test*
85	
86	**KR3 (commit)** — 100% of drained issues in the window invoke the new execution skills and zero invoke superpowers/agent-skills; both packs uninstalled and zero references to their skill names remain in the Shaper repo or drain-cycle prompts *(brake)*
87	- baseline: both packs installed; `drain_cycle/prompt.py` hardcodes `/code-review-and-quality`
88	- target: 100% first-party invocation, 0 third-party references, 0 packs installed
89	- measured over: by cycle close
90	- how we'll know: `grep -rn 'code-review-and-quality\|superpowers' drain_cycle/ skills/ .claude/` returns empty + worker transcripts in run logs show only first-party skill invocations + plugin list output
91	
92	*Layer 1: discipline · Layer 2: artefact-exists / structural check*
93	
94	*Dimensions: outcome / correctness / discipline — all distinct per rule 5b.*
95	
96	**Affected repos:** agent-skills-shaper, drain-cycle (one-line prompt pointer only)
97	
98	**Appetite:** ~10 issues
99	
100	**Kill condition:** If, after the execution skills ship, 3 consecutive drained issues require manual fix commits or fall back to third-party pack skills, stop — reinstall agent-skills, keep Shaper shaping-only, and fold the learnings into the pack's README.
101	
102	**Project type:** 1 — methodology skill pack
103	
104	**Dominant model tier:** Balanced — most issues are bounded skill-authoring against existing anatomy specs; the persona-contract and namespace decisions are the Frontier exceptions.
105	
```

20	
21	# N01 — Front-door IA design doc
22	
23	**Type:** `design-doc` · lands in the **agent-skills-shaper** repo. The Rule A1 node for D1:
24	the absorption map is a one-way door (folded skills get deleted) published through shared
25	install infrastructure, so it is decided once, in a reviewed doc, before any door is built.
26	
27	> **Blocked by:** initiative A's
28	> [N04](../../issues-drain-end-to-end/D2-execution-workflow-drains-issues/N04-execution-verb-namespace-design-doc.md) —
29	> A's design doc decides the verb-namespace naming table; this doc consumes that table so the
30	> `shape:*` doors and A's execution verbs are named from one scheme, not two.
31	
32	> **▶ On pickup:** author the doc via the `design-doc` skill; N02–N06's task breakdowns wait
33	> until it is accepted.
34	
35	## What
36	
37	Decide the information architecture the four doors are built against: which sections of
38	idea-triage, app-calibrate, initiative-shape, roadmap-shape, backlog-manage, design-doc,
39	backend-spike, and product-spike fold into which door, and which content is deliberately
40	deleted with a written rationale; the survivor manifest (4 doors + ≤3 utilities) and **the
41	measured set for the ≤2,200-line check** — initiative A is concurrently adding execution
42	skills to `skills/`, so `wc -l skills/*/SKILL.md` drifts for reasons outside this initiative
43	and the doc must pin which files KR1 counts; the frontmatter-cut spec (which of the current
44	ten fields survive, against `docs/skill-anatomy.md`); and the door directory / command-wrapper
45	/ `shape-*` symlink / `using-this-pack` route naming, consuming A/N04's naming table.
46	
47	## Why
48	
49	The bet: one reviewed IA decision makes the four door builds mechanical compression jobs
50	instead of four separate judgement calls that drift apart. Rejected alternative: let each door
51	node decide its own absorption inline — that re-decides the same one-way door four times, and
52	a dropped `[GATE]` in one fold surfaces only at the benchmark, not at review. Unblocks N02–N06
53	(their task breakdowns wait on this doc) and gives N08 the deletion list it sweeps against.
54	
55	## Completion
56	
57	Prose form (design-doc): the accepted doc covers the absorption map for all 14 baseline
58	skills, the survivor manifest plus the measured set for the cap check, the frontmatter-cut
59	spec, the naming scheme consumed from A/N04's table, and the gate-preservation inventory —
60	every `[GATE]` line in a folded skill mapped to a destination section or a written deletion
61	decision.
62	
63	## Assumptions
64	
65	- A/N04's naming table is accepted before this node is picked up. *(verified — enforced by the blockedBy edge onto ABA-361; cycle planning cannot pull this node first)*
66	- KR1's measured set excludes the execution skills initiative A adds to `skills/`. *(to-verify — the KR baseline counted the pre-A 14-skill set; the doc pins the measured set explicitly and the owner confirms it at doc acceptance, rather than the KR being silently rewritten)*
67	- `plan-review`, `render-html`, and `using-this-pack` are the ≤3 utilities. *(verified — the confirmed intent names plan-review and render-html as survivors at `docs/ideas/lifecycle-expansion.md:40`, and `using-this-pack` is the router the doors are reached through)*
68	
69	## Key Risks
70	
71	- **Risk:** the absorption map silently drops a `[GATE]` from a folded skill, and the loss is
72	  discovered only when the benchmark or a live shaping run misbehaves.
73	  *Mitigation:* the gate-preservation inventory is a named task with its own Done-when — every
74	  `[GATE]` in the eight folded/compressed skills is enumerated and mapped before the doc is
75	  accepted.
76	- **Risk:** the doc scope creeps into authoring the doors' prose inline.
77	  *Mitigation:* the doc fixes maps, manifests, and naming only; door prose is N02–N05's work
78	  at pickup — same boundary delivery-shape itself draws (agentic P7).
79	
80	## Tasks
81	
82	- [ ] `skeleton` — Draft the IA doc via the design-doc skill (absorption map, survivor manifest + measured set, frontmatter-cut spec, naming consumed from A/N04's table folded in) · Done when: the doc exists under docs/designs/ with all 14 baseline skills mapped to fold / delete / survive · Model: Frontier · risk one-way door · review elevated · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = H·H·L·H·L · companions code-review-and-quality
83	- [ ] Compile the gate-preservation inventory and fold it into the doc · Done when: every `[GATE]` line in the eight folded/compressed skills appears in the doc mapped to a destination section or a written deletion rationale · Model: Balanced · risk reversible · review standard · axes RC·SC·HS·SR·OR = M·M·L·M·L
84

id: toolu_01GkTU4o8TVW2z6wKkJFFDmU
```
1	# Skill anatomy
2	
3	The authoring spec for `SKILL.md` files in `skills/<name>/`.
4	
5	## Frontmatter
6	
7	```yaml
8	---
9	name: <skill-name>
10	description: <1-2 sentences. State what the skill does, then "Use when …", then trigger phrases.>
11	---
12	```
13	
14	Taxonomy the dropped fields used to carry (pack, lifecycle stage, predecessor) is preserved in the README tables and git history, not in the file.
15	
16	## Sections
17	
18	In this exact order:
19	
20	1. **Title** — `# <Skill Name>`
21	2. **Purpose** — one or two plain sentences: what the skill does and the one thing that must hold. No "without it…" doom paragraph, no inline principle citations.
22	3. **When to use** — tight bullets; explicit intents, with the size-the-work rubric where relevant
23	4. **Do not use when** — tight bullets; explicit non-triggers, each naming the right alternative inline
24	5. **Inputs** — what the skill expects to be given
25	6. **Outputs** — what it produces, including any artefact path; show the record/verdict shape as a fenced `json` block where the skill emits one
26	7. **Workflow** — `### N. <step>` headers. A hard gate is a `### N. Gate: <name>` header — the gate semantics are mandatory; only the literal `[GATE]` marker is replaced by the `Gate:` heading
27	8. **Artefact template** — the markdown (or JSON) the skill produces, as a fenced block
28	9. **Red flags** — bullet list of signs the skill is being violated
29	10. **Exit criteria** — how to know the skill ran correctly
30	11. **Related** — plain links to sibling skills and references; no principle-citation footnotes
31	
32	## Length
33	
34	Target 100–300 lines. Below 100, editor checks for under-specification (acceptable if genuinely complete). Above 300, editor checks for redundancy with other skills, scope creep, or over-explanation. Hard cap 350 lines; beyond that, split.
35	
36	## Artefact template section name
37	
38	The anatomy calls this section "Artefact template." Skills that produce a single named artefact may use a descriptive variant (e.g. "Task list template", "Finding template") — the name should identify the artefact type. The section must still appear in the required position.
39	
40	## Meta-pack skills
41	
42	Navigation and orientation skills (e.g. `using-this-pack`) are not workflow skills. They are exempt from the body sections Inputs, Outputs, Artefact template, Red flags, and Exit criteria. They must still have: Title, Purpose, When to use, Do not use when, and a Related or equivalent lookup table.
43	
44	## See also
45	
46	- `docs/hook-anatomy.md` — for hooks
47	- `docs/sub-agent-anatomy.md` — for sub-agent personas
48	- `docs/brief-template.md` — for the brief that drives authoring
49	- `docs/authoring-learnings.md` — calibration guidance and failure modes
50	
```

Absorption map — all 14 baseline skills\n\nDisposition vocabulary: **fold** (content moves into a door section), **absorb** (a clause or fragment moves; the section dies), **delete** (dies with written rationale), **survive** (file continues, possibly re-housed), **transfer** (leaves the shaping half for initiative A's execution half).\n\nA door's body follows the eleven-section anatomy (Title → Purpose → When to use → Do not use when → Inputs → Outputs → Workflow → Artefact template → Red flags → Exit criteria → Related). Rule: load-bearing rows merge into the door's **Red flags**; the rest is explanatory redundancy and is deleted. plan-review and render-html keep theirs until their own density passes apply the same rule.\n- **\"References\" sections** become the door's **Related** section, citing only references the door actually uses.\n\n**idea-triage (220) → fold into `shape:idea`** — the door's core.\n\n| Source section | Disposition |\n|---|---|\n| Purpose / When to use / When not to use | fold → door Purpose / When to use / Do not use when; the semantics both sources share — a spike may not end without a written finding — are preserved once |\n\nFor completeness, gates outside the fold (not at risk, listed so N08 has the full ledger): delivery-shape 1 (carried by the re-house, count must stay 1), plan-review 6 (kept verbatim through N06's compression), render-html 3 (untouched), using-this-pack 0, pr-prepare 2 + verify-implementation 2 (transfer with their skills).\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Positive.**\n\n- N02–N05 become mechanical compression jobs against one spec; their Done-whens check `wc -l`, gate headers vs this inventory, and `install.sh` resolution — no per-door judgement calls.\n- N08 gets its deletion list (7 skill dirs, 7 wrappers, 2 orphaned references, 2 dangling citations) from one table.\n- Gate loss becomes a review-time diff, not a benchmark-time surprise.\n- The naming rule is a convention (wrapper leaf = dir leaf = frontmatter name), not a lookup table.\n\n**Negative / costs.**\n\n- The door names are themselves a one-way door: once `using-this-pack`, README, and initiative C's prompts cite `/shape:idea` etc., a rename is a coordinated sweep. Accepted — that is exactly why this doc, not a door node, decides them.\n- Dedicated curation and portfolio ceremony is gone; if a real planning-cycle need emerges, it returns as a new shaped initiative, not a resurrection of the deleted files (git history keeps them recoverable).\n- `shape:design` carries fold risk (568 → ≤350 hard) — mitigated by the kill-condition watch note on N05, with this doc's inventory as the non-negotiable floor: a fold that closes under 350 only by dropping a gate is a kill, not a ship.\n\n**Walking skeleton (Rule B2).** Required and already scheduled: **N02 (`shape:delivery` re-house) is the skeleton.** It exercises every seam this doc decides — directory move, wrapper retarget, symlink regeneration and prune, router route, frontmatter cut — on the one skill with no absorption risk, before any content fold starts. N03–N05 build only after the skeleton proves the plumbing. (Q1 notes the one scope correction the skeleton inherits.)\n\n## Operability plan\n\nAdapted to a Markdown-only pack: \"production\" is every installed copy of the pack, and the fitness functions are deterministic shell checks.\n\n- **Metrics.** Per door node at Done-when: file line count; gate-header count vs this inventory; `install.sh` re-run resolving the new verb; both `bin/` plan gates exiting 0 (N02). Initiative-level: measured-set total (N06); benchmark pass rate (N07); legacy-citation grep count (N08).\n- **Structured logs.** Each door PR body records the before/after line counts and the gate diff against the inventory table — the review trail KR1's drains grep for.\n- **Alerts (kill conditions, owner-routed).** Door >350 lines without dropping a gate → stop, surface to the owner, ship style reforms only (project brake). Benchmark <80% after two repair attempts (N07) → same brake. Both are written into the project description; nodes cite them.\n- **Rollback plan.** Each door + its source deletions land as one revertible unit. Steps: (1) `git revert` the door commit — restores the source skills and wrappers atomically; (2) re-run `install.sh` — regenerates wrappers and prunes the door's now-stale `shape-*` symlink (verified prune behaviour, `install.sh:77–84`); (3) verification gate: the old slash commands resolve and the door's verb is gone.\n- **Capacity headroom.** 1,951-line worst-case projection vs the 2,200 cap → ≥249 lines (≈11%) headroom before the brake trips.\n- **Known failure modes.** (a) Silent gate loss in a fold → the inventory is a Done-when input, and N08 sweeps stragglers. (b) Fold overruns the cap → N05's watch note + kill condition. (c) Baseline drift between this doc and pickup (already happened once: delivery-shape 266→381) → every door node re-measures its sources at pickup and records a baseline-refresh note when numbers moved. (d) Stale wrappers/symlinks after deletion → `install.sh` prune + N08 grep. (e) Concurrent initiative-A churn in `skills/` → measured set is pinned by filename, immune to additions.\n- **Upstream dependencies.** `install.sh` wrapper/symlink generation; `docs/skill-anatomy.md` (the frontmatter/body spec of record); ADR 0004 (verbs). A change to any of these reopens this doc by ADR.\n- **Downstream dependencies.** N02–N06 Done-whens; N08's sweep list; initiative C's supervisor prompts (will cite door verbs literally — they bind after this doc is accepted, which is what makes the naming a one-way door); `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` and the example plans (path citations updated in N08).\n\n## Open questions\n\n| Q | Owner | Resolution gate |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Q1.** delivery-shape is 381 lines (hard cap 350, target 300) — N02 was scoped as \"re-house only, no compression\" when the file was 266. Does N02's density-pass task absorb the −81-line cut, or does a separate task get added to N02 at its breakdown? (This doc's recommendation: absorb into N02's existing \"frontmatter cut and density pass\" task — the task already exists, only its size changed.) | Anton, at doc acceptance | N02's at-pickup task breakdown — blocked until answered |\n| **Q2.** Orphaned/dangling references: delete `portfolio-themes.md` and `dora-metrics.md`, and strip (vs restore) render-html's citations to the two non-existent `html-*.md` files? (Default per this doc: delete the orphans; strip the dangling citations unless the files are recovered from git history first.) | N08 pickup | N08's sweep task Done-when |\n| **Q3.** Confirm the measured set: KR1's baseline counted the pre-initiative-A 14-skill set; this doc pins the measured set to the seven survivor files above, excluding the transferred pr-prepare and verify-implementation. Per N01's to-verify assumption, the owner confirms at acceptance rather than the KR being silently rewritten. | Anton, at doc acceptance | this doc's acceptance — N06's footprint check binds to the answer |\n\n## Review record\n\n*(populated at the plan-review exit gate)*\n"
}

All verification complete. Here are the findings.

---

## Findings — adversarial review of docs/designs/front-door-ia.md

### Verified and held (not reported in detail, per instructions)
All 14 baseline line counts; the 2,599 sum; all 19 gate line numbers and gate texts (G1–G19 every one line-accurate); the 15-mapped/4-deleted split; the out-of-fold gate counts for plan-review (6), render-html (3), initiative-shape (3), pr-prepare (2), verify-implementation (2); portfolio-themes cited by no skill except roadmap-shape; dora-metrics cited by zero skills; kano-classification cited only by roadmap-shape; frontmatter field counts 10/10/6; the full wrapper inventory (all 14 `.claude/commands/*.md` accounted for, no app-calibrate wrapper exists); install.sh prune behaviour at :77–84.

### BLOCKING

**B1 — The doc's "dangling citation" fact is false; Q2's default action would vandalize a working skill.**
- Section: §4 render-html row, Reference disposition, Q2, Consequences ("2 dangling citations").
- Claim attacked: render-html "cites `references/html-patterns.md` and `references/html-skeleton.md`, **neither of which exists in the repo** — restore the files or strip the citations."
- Evidence: both files exist — `/Users/anton/src/agent-skills-shaper/skills/render-html/references/html-patterns.md` (1,060+ lines) and `.../html-skeleton.md`. The skill cites them as bundled skill-local references, not repo-root: `skills/render-html/SKILL.md:74,77,87,125–126` use `<skill-base>/references/...`. `docs/tasks/render-html.md:20,32` show they were deliberately built at that path.
- Consequences: Q2's default ("strip the dangling citations") deletes load-bearing citations from a working skill; the N08 deletion-list count ("2 dangling citations") is wrong; and "render-html → survive, unchanged except frontmatter cut" silently carries a bundled `references/` subdirectory the IA never mentions or pins in the measured set.
- Falsifying condition: `ls skills/render-html/references/` — already falsified.

**B2 — The "−81-line density pass" on delivery-shape assumes redundancy; the git record shows deliberate, days-old contract expansion.**
- Section: Context (baseline drift bullet), §4 delivery-shape row, Q1, Operability failure mode (c).
- Claim attacked: 381→300 is achievable as a "density pass" absorbed into N02's existing task.
- Evidence: at `cfe42f6` (the anatomy prune the doc itself cites as the 2026-06-12 spec of record) delivery-shape was **196 lines** (`git show cfe42f6:skills/delivery-shape/SKILL.md | wc -l`). The owner then deliberately grew it to 381 via `ac931a7` (skeleton conditional on node type/risk locus — a semantic rule) and `372f09f`/`fc9b497` (`git diff cfe42f6..HEAD --stat`: +293/−108). The added content is normative contract — `Done when:` gate, size-check, model-routing rubric, `⚠ blocks on:` flags — enforced by `bin/check-plan-framing` (per the `45b49b9` commit message and the current step-9 text). The doc's drift attribution is also wrong: `45b49b9` *predates* `cfe42f6` and contributed only +36/−8; the 266→381 narrative misattributes the growth.
- Falsifying condition: a trial compression that reaches ≤300 without deleting Done-when/Model:/skeleton-conditionality prose. If it can't, N02's "walking skeleton with no absorption risk" inherits a kill-condition fight on day one.

**B3 — The gate-preservation fitness function is blind to delivery-shape's de-marked gates.**
- Section: §5 footnote ("delivery-shape 1 … count must stay 1") and the NFR "Gate preservation … grep of gate headers".
- Claim attacked: counting gate markers protects delivery-shape's gate semantics through the re-house and density pass.
- Evidence: `45b49b9` introduced `**7c. [GATE] Acceptance criterion…**` and `**7d. [GATE] Size check…**`; the current 381-line file has **no** `[GATE]` markers in step 7 (`grep -niE 'gate' skills/delivery-shape/SKILL.md` → only line 82) — the restructure de-marked them while keeping the substance at lines ~202–312. A density pass policed by "marker count stays 1" can delete the AC and size-check gate semantics and pass every check in this doc.
- Falsifying condition: the inventory either re-marks those sub-steps as gates pre-fold or names their prose as protected content; otherwise gate loss here is exactly the "benchmark-time surprise" the doc claims to prevent.

**B4 — Undeclared scope: `bin/eval-triggers` hardcodes the names being deleted/renamed.**
- Section: Constraints (only `bin/walk-delivery-plan` and `bin/check-plan-framing` are protected); nothing in the doc mentions this script.
- Evidence: `bin/eval-triggers:32–35` — `CANDIDATE_SKILLS=("delivery-shape" "initiative-shape")` — plus `EVAL_FILE="$REPO_ROOT/eval/delivery-shape-triggers.md"` (file exists at `eval/delivery-shape-triggers.md`). Deleting `skills/initiative-shape/` and moving `skills/delivery-shape/` → `skills/delivery/` breaks the trigger-eval harness silently; no node owns the fix and the NFR greps don't cover `bin/` or `eval/`.

### CLARITY

**C1 — The worst-case arithmetic is wrong twice.**
- Section: §2 and Operability "Capacity headroom".
- Claim: "300×5 + 131 + ~120 = **1,951** ≤ 2,200 (≥249 headroom)". Actual: 300×5 + 131 + 120 = **1,751**, headroom ≥449. The error repeats in the Operability plan ("1,951 … ≥249 (≈11%)"). Direction is conservative, but two load-bearing numbers are wrong — and a true worst case should model `shape:design` at the 350 hard cap the doc elsewhere lets ship flagged (→ 1,801, headroom 399).

**C2 — Undeclared scope: install.sh's hardcoded help text refutes "install.sh derives everything else."**
- Section: §1 naming-schema rules; Context cites only `install.sh` :20–38, :77–84, :86–93.
- Evidence: `install.sh:162–173` hardcodes echo lines for `/shape:idea-triage`, `/shape:roadmap`, `/shape:product-spike`, `/shape:backend-spike`, `/shape:design-doc`, `/shape:delivery`, `/shape:initiative`, `/shape:backlog-manage`. N02's skeleton claims to "exercise every seam" but this seam isn't listed; between N02 and N08 every install prints dead commands. The NFR grep does cover install.sh, so it surfaces eventually — but no node before N08 owns it and the "derives everything" claim is false.

**C3 — Undeclared scope: `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` description names deleted capabilities.**
- Evidence: `plugin.json` description: "Covers idea triage, roadmap shaping, prototyping, design docs…". The manifest is named in the doc's trigger sentence but appears in no disposition row, no NFR grep scope (README/install.sh/hooks/using-this-pack/.claude/commands only), and not in N08's list.

**C4 — Undeclared scope: `docs/designs/shaping-pipeline.md` is the prior IA and is never disposed of.**
- Evidence: 14 legacy-name citations; status "Proposed", 2026-06-03 — it describes the 14-skill pipeline this doc replaces, yet front-door-ia's frontmatter says `supersedes: none` and the sweep scope excludes `docs/designs/`. Same gap, smaller: `docs/idea-bank/triage-baseline-data.md` (3 hits) and `docs/brief-template.md` (idea-bank flow).

**C5 — "Two skills sit over the 300-line cap" — there are three.**
- Section: Problem. `roadmap-shape` is 303 lines; the doc's own anchor (`docs/ideas/lifecycle-expansion.md`: "3 over-cap skills brought under 300") counts three. Immaterial to the outcome (roadmap-shape dies) but the problem statement is factually wrong and contradicts its cited source.

### NIT

**N1** — `skills/using-this-pack/SKILL.md:60` contains the literal string `[GATE]` in prose; the doc records using-this-pack as 0 gates (true) but N08's straggler grep for gate markers will false-positive there unless the sweep spec says so.

**N2** — CHANGELOG carries 8 legacy-name hits and is excluded from the zero-citation grep; presumably intentional (history is history) but the doc never says so, while it does cite CHANGELOG when discussing dora-metrics.

---

**Verdict: REVISE.** The gate inventory, naming schema, and absorption map are solid — every line number and count I attacked held. But the doc ships one outright false repo fact that drives a destructive default (B1), rests its delivery-shape plan on a density-pass premise the git record contradicts (B2/B3), and misses a real breakage in `bin/eval-triggers` plus three stale-citation surfaces outside its sweep scope. Fix those before N02–N05 build against it; do not kill it.
