Before any compression starts, Q4 settles the six de-marked gates — re-mark them, or record the demotion as deliberate — so the gate fitness check has a true baseline.\n\n**plan-review (369) → survive** as a utility. N06 compresses it to ≤300 as pure density reform: all six `[GATE]` steps stay verbatim, `evals/evals.json` stays byte-identical, and the tier-selection and B1–B8 axes do not change. The frontmatter cut applies.\n\n**render-html (131) → survive** as a utility, unchanged except the frontmatter cut. Its references are **bundled skill-local files** (`skills/render-html/references/html-patterns.md`, `html-skeleton.md`) cited via `<skill-base>/references/…` — they exist, travel with the directory, and stay outside the measured set.\n\n**using-this-pack (119) → survive** as the router. N06 rewrites it to route exactly the four doors plus the utilities, point execution work at `exec:pickup`, and stay within **≤150 lines**. The frontmatter cut applies (currently 6 fields). It carries no gates; the meta-pack anatomy exemptions apply. N06 rephrases line 60's prose explanation of the `[GATE]` convention — or the sweep whitelists it — so N08's straggler grep stays clean.\n\n**pr-prepare (163) and verify-implementation (123) → transfer** to the execution half. ADR 0004 reserves their successors (`exec:finish`, `exec:verify`); initiative A owns the skills, their gates (2 + 2), and their wrappers. They leave the shaping measured set; N08 sweeps only their shaping-side citations.\n\n**Reference disposition** (the map settles these too). Survive: app-context-schema, confidence-meter, ice-scoring, kano-classification, initiative-types, kr-quality-templates, task-sizing, nfr-categories, plus render-html's bundled pair. Orphaned, onto N08's deletion list: portfolio-themes (only roadmap-shape cites it) and dora-metrics (no skill cites it; README/CHANGELOG only).\n\n### 5. Gate inventory — every gate in the eight folded skills\n\nThe eight folded skills carry 19 gates at the 2026-06-12 baseline (line numbers from that baseline): 15 map to destinations, 4 die with rationale. N03–N05's Done-whens check door gate headers against this table, not reviewer memory.\n\n| # | Source gate | Destination / deletion rationale |\n|---|---|---|\n| G1 | idea-triage:83 — step 2 \"Diagnose: problem or solution?\" | `shape:idea` Workflow — early gate, carried verbatim |\n| G2 | idea-triage:105 — step 5 \"Does the problem statement hold?\" | `shape:idea` Workflow — carried verbatim |\n| G3 | idea-triage:118 — step 7 \"Route the idea.\" | `shape:idea` Workflow — final routing gate, carried; gains two absorbed clauses (G4 grounding, G12 confirm-writes) |\n| G4 | app-calibrate:79 — step 4 \"Minimum validity check.\" | **absorbed** as a clause on `shape:idea`'s scoring/evidence gate: an ICE Impact score that cites no `app-context.md` baseline is marked ungrounded |\n| G5 | initiative-shape:43 — gate 2 \"problem or solution?\" | `shape:project` Workflow — carried verbatim |\n| G6 | initiative-shape:94 — gate 7 \"user confirmation\" | `shape:project` Workflow — carried verbatim (also the surviving home of tracker-write confirmation) |\n| G7 | initiative-shape:100 — gate 8 \"verification rubric — 11 cross-cutting rules\" | `shape:project` Workflow — carried verbatim; rubric content untouched |\n| G8 | roadmap-shape:108 — step 3 \"Strip any solution framing.\" | **deleted** — duplicate: the identical check survives twice, as G1 (`shape:idea`) and G5 (`shape:project`); every path to a roadmap-shaped artefact now passes one of them |\n| G9 | roadmap-shape:129 — step 6 \"Confidence gate (Rule B6).\" | **deleted** — G3 supersedes it: `shape:idea`'s routing gate already forces low-confidence ideas to the validate lane against `references/confidence-meter.md` (which survives); no second moment exists where the check would fire |\n| G10 | roadmap-shape:175 — step 12 \"Shape review.\" | **deleted** — review-of-a-roadmap is plan-review's When-to-use, and plan-review survives; the ceremony around it dies with the skill |\n| G11 | backlog-manage:103 — step 3 \"Identify session intent.\" | **deleted** — the gate disambiguated the multi-mode curation session; the modes die with the skill, and intake disambiguation is `shape:idea`'s When-to-use |\n| G12 | backlog-manage:123 — step 5 \"Confirm writes.\" | **absorbed** as a clause on G3: any route that mutates the idea bank or the tracker announces the write and confirms it first |\n| G13 | design-doc:46 — gate 1 \"trigger check\" | `shape:design` Workflow — carried, extended into the typed branch point (doc vs technical spike vs product spike); the no-trigger → ADR referral is unchanged |\n| G14 | design-doc:54 — gate 3 \"problem section\" | `shape:design` doc branch — carried verbatim |\n| G15 | design-doc:74 — gate 8 \"operability plan\" | `shape:design` doc branch — carried verbatim |\n| G16 | backend-spike:80 — gate 1 \"Write the question.\" | `shape:design` spike branch — carried, shared across both spike variants |\n| G17 | backend-spike:105 — gate 4 \"Scope check.\" | `shape:design` spike branch — carried (throwaway scope + timebox discipline) |\n| G18 | backend-spike:114 — gate 6 \"Write the recommendation.\" | `shape:design` spike branch — exit gate, merged with G19 into one \"write the finding/recommendation\" gate over the merged spike record template |\n| G19 | product-spike:104 — gate 5 \"Write the finding.\" | merged into the same spike exit gate as G18; both sources share one semantic — a spike may not end without a written finding — and the merge preserves it once |\n\n**The delivery-shape ledger — gates outside the fold but inside the risk.** The re-house must not inherit the marker count of an already-degraded baseline. From `45b49b9`, `shape:delivery`'s ledger is: D1 committed-input (still marked, line 82 — carried); D2 map-to-KRs (de-marked, substance at step 3); D3 Rule A1 design-first (de-marked, step 4); D4 five-section body (de-marked, step 6); D5 acceptance criterion on every task (de-marked, step-7 rules); D6 size check (de-marked, step-7 rules); D7 verify-plan (de-marked, step 9). Q4 decides re-mark vs recorded demotion; either way N02's Done-when checks against **this ledger of seven**, not against \"count stays 1\".\n\nThe gates elsewhere, listed so N08 holds the full ledger (none at risk): plan-review 6 (kept verbatim through N06's compression), render-html 3 (untouched), using-this-pack 0 (one prose mention of the convention; see the NFR note), pr-prepare 2 and verify-implementation 2 (transfer with their skills).\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Positive.**\n\n- N02–N05 become mechanical compression jobs against one spec; their Done-whens check `wc -l`, gate headers against this inventory, and `install.sh` resolution — no per-door judgement calls.\n- N08 reads its deletion/cleanup list from one place: 7 skill dirs, 7 wrappers, 2 orphaned references, the install.sh help-text block, the plugin-manifest description, and the `bin/eval-triggers` / `eval/` retargets.\n- Gate loss becomes a review-time diff, not a benchmark-time surprise — and the one silent loss that already happened (delivery-shape) is now surfaced and tracked instead of inherited.\n- The naming rule stays a convention (wrapper leaf = dir leaf = frontmatter name), not a lookup table.\n\n**Negative / costs.**\n\n- The door names are themselves a one-way door: once `using-this-pack`, README, and initiative C's prompts cite `/shape:idea` and friends, a rename costs a coordinated sweep. Accepted — that is exactly why this doc, not a door node, decides them, and why ADR 0005 records them at acceptance.\n- Dedicated curation and portfolio ceremony dies. If a real planning-cycle need emerges, it returns as a new shaped initiative, not a resurrection of the deleted files (git history keeps them recoverable).\n- `shape:design` carries fold risk (568 → ≤350 hard) — mitigated by the kill-condition watch note on N05, with this doc's inventory as the non-negotiable floor: a fold that closes under 350 only by dropping a gate is a kill, not a ship.\n- `shape:delivery` is no longer the zero-risk skeleton N02 assumed: the contract-aware compression (Q1) adds real work to the walking-skeleton node. Accepted trade-off — the alternative (skeleton on a different door) couples plumbing risk to fold risk, which is worse.\n\n**Walking skeleton (Rule B2).** Required and already scheduled: **N02 (`shape:delivery` re-house) is the skeleton.** It exercises every seam this doc decides — directory move, wrapper retarget, symlink regeneration and prune, install.sh help-text edit, `bin/eval-triggers` retarget, router route, frontmatter cut — on the one door with no *absorption* risk, before any content fold starts. N03–N05 build only after the skeleton proves the plumbing. (Q1 and Q4 record the scope corrections the skeleton inherits.)\n\n## Operability plan\n\nAdapted to a Markdown-only pack: \"production\" is every installed copy of the pack, and the fitness functions are deterministic shell checks.\n\n- **Metrics.** Per door node at Done-when: the file's line count; its gate-header count against this inventory (including the delivery ledger); an `install.sh` re-run resolving the new verb; both `bin/` plan gates exiting 0 and `bin/eval-triggers` running (N02). Initiative-level: the measured-set total (N06); the benchmark pass rate (N07); the legacy-citation grep count over the live surfaces (N08).\n- **Structured logs.** Each door PR body records the before/after line counts and the gate diff against the inventory table — the review trail KR1's drains grep for.\n- **Alerts (kill conditions, owner-routed).** A door exceeds 350 lines without dropping a gate → stop, surface to the owner, ship style reforms only (project brake). The benchmark drops below 80% after two repair attempts (N07) → same brake. The project description records both; nodes cite them.\n- **Rollback plan.** Each door plus its source deletions lands as one revertible unit. Steps: (1) `git revert` the door commit — restores the source skills and wrappers atomically; (2) re-run `install.sh` — regenerates wrappers and prunes the door's now-stale `shape-*` symlink (prune behaviour verified at `install.sh:77–84`); (3) verification gate: the old slash commands resolve and the door's verb is gone.\n- **Capacity headroom.** The 1,831-line worst case (shape:design modelled at its flagged 350) against the 2,200 cap leaves ≥369 lines (≈17%) before the brake trips.\n- **Known failure modes.** (a) A fold silently drops a gate → the inventory and the delivery ledger are Done-when inputs, and N08 sweeps stragglers. (b) A fold overruns the cap → N05's watch note plus the kill condition. (c) The baseline drifts between this doc and pickup (it already happened once: delivery-shape 196→381 between issue-writing and pickup) → every door node re-measures its sources at pickup and records a baseline-refresh note when the numbers moved. (d) Stale wrappers, symlinks, or help text outlive a deletion → the `install.sh` prune, the per-node help-text edit, and the N08 grep. (e) Initiative A churns `skills/` concurrently → the measured set is pinned by filename, immune to additions. (f) Compression deletes normative contract from shape:delivery → the protected-content list in Constraints, the `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` overflow route, and a `bin/check-plan-framing` re-run.\n- **Upstream dependencies.** `install.sh` wrapper/symlink generation and help text; `docs/skill-anatomy.md` (the frontmatter/body spec of record); ADR 0004 (verbs); `bin/eval-triggers` plus the `eval/` fixtures. A change to any of these reopens this doc by ADR.\n- **Downstream dependencies.** N02–N06 Done-whens; N08's sweep list; initiative C's supervisor prompts (they will cite door verbs literally — they bind after this doc is accepted, which is what makes the naming a one-way door); `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` and the example plans (N08 updates their path citations); `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (N06 rewrites the description).\n\n## Open questions\n\n| Q | Owner | Resolution gate |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Q1.** shape:delivery sizing. The 381 lines include deliberate, recent contract content (196 at `cfe42f6` → 381 via `ac931a7`/`372f09f`), so a density pass alone cannot reach ≤300. Confirm, or raise shape:delivery's cap to \"≤350 flagged\" instead? Either way, N02's first task classifies the `cfe42f6..HEAD` diff into protected vs compressible lines. | Anton, at doc acceptance | N02's at-pickup task breakdown — blocked until answered |\n| **Q2.** Orphaned references: delete `references/portfolio-themes.md` and `references/dora-metrics.md` once roadmap-shape dies? (Default per this doc: delete both; no skill cites dora-metrics today.) | N08 pickup | N08's sweep task Done-when |\n| **Q3.** Confirm the measured set. KR1's baseline counted the pre-initiative-A 14-skill set; this doc pins the measured set to the seven survivor files above, excluding the transferred pr-prepare and verify-implementation. Per N01's to-verify assumption, the owner confirms at acceptance rather than the KR being silently rewritten. | Anton, at doc acceptance | this doc's acceptance — N06's footprint check binds to the answer |\n| **Q4.** delivery-shape's six de-marked gates (ledger D2–D7): re-mark them as `### N. 11	      "body": "re-write this plan using the following guidelines\n\nWriting Guidelines for AI Agents\nUse these rules for software engineering and product management writing.\nCore Rules\nPut the actor in the subject and the action in the verb. Prefer \"The API caches responses\" over \"Caching is implemented by the API.\"\nUse concrete verbs. Replace nominalizations such as \"implementation,\" \"utilization,\" and \"optimization\" with \"implement,\" \"use,\" and \"optimize.\"\nMake each goal, milestone, or task produce a visible work product: a shipped change, design doc, verified fix, report, migration, test, or decision.\nStart with known context and end with the new or important information.\nCut filler, but keep assumptions, risks, constraints, dependencies, owners, dates, and success criteria.\nWrite like a professional colleague. Prefer a clear story over a list of disconnected facts.\nProject Goals and OKRs\nExplain the gap between the current state, the desired state, and the cost of staying where you are.\nUse Situation, Complication, Question, Answer logic internally, but do not use those labels as headings.\nMake success measurable.\nBad:\n\"Improve the efficiency of our API and utilize better caching.\"\nGood:\n\"Our API serves 1k RPS today. The Q3 launch requires 10k RPS, which will exceed current capacity. We will re-architect the load balancer to support 15k RPS.\"\nBad:\n\"Significantly improve dashboard speed.\"\nGood:\n\"Reduce P95 dashboard load time from 4.5s to under 800ms using Edge Workers.\"\nDesign Docs\nProblem: Define the current state and the cost of the status quo.\nContext: Anchor the reader in stable facts before introducing the change or conflict.\nConstraints: Group non-functional requirements by structure or impact.\nAlternatives: Compare distinct options and state the practical effect of each one.\nRecommended approach: Start with what the team already knows, then introduce the proposed change.\nOperability plan: Define verifiable rollback gates, monitoring signals, and ownership.\nMilestones and Deliverables\nUse milestones as summary achievements, not labels such as \"Phase 1\" or \"Research.\"\nGroup work into non-overlapping containers.\nOrder groups by sequence, system structure, or impact.\nBad:\n\"Frontend; Data; Implementation.\"\nGood:\n\"Built responsive React components; Migrated user records to a sharded Postgres cluster.\"\nBad:\n\"Security; Authentication; Login Page.\"\nGood:\n\"Identity management; Data encryption at rest.\"\nTasks, Stories, Bugs, and Refactors\nEvery task needs a concrete \"done when\" state.\nAvoid blank tasks such as \"Investigate latency\" unless the output is named.\nUse active voice.\nBad:\n\"Perform an investigation into the latency situation.\"\nGood:\n\"Done when: The report identifies the three highest-latency queries in the /orders endpoint.\"\nBad:\n\"As a user, I want the utilization of an automated system.\"\nGood:\n\"As a user, I want to reset my password through an email link so I can regain access in under 2 minutes.\"\nVocabulary\nUse exact technical terms when they are accurate. Replace metaphors only when they hide the cause.\nPrefer \"essential\" or \"required\" over vague intensifiers such as \"vital.\"\nReplace \"bottleneck\" with the specific shortage, delay, queue, lock, or capacity limit when known.\nPrefer \"plan\" or \"spec\" over \"blueprint\" unless discussing an actual final design.\nPrefer \"limit\" or \"maximum\" over \"ceiling.\"\nPrefer \"use\" over \"utilization.\"\nPrefer \"implement\" over \"implementation\" when describing action.\nPrefer \"communicate with,\" \"connect to,\" or \"call\" over \"interface with.\"\nPrefer \"affect\" over \"impact\" as a verb.\nDelete \"basically,\" \"actually,\" \"quite,\" \"it should be noted that,\" and similar throat-clearing.\nReplace \"at this point in time\" with \"now.\"\nAgent Review Checklist\nBefore returning an artifact, check:\nDoes it move from problem to solution in a clear order?\nDo subtasks explain how to achieve the milestone or why the milestone matters?\nDid pruning preserve assumptions, risks, dependencies, constraints, and success criteria?\nIs every task's done state visible and verifiable?\nDo important terms or conclusions appear where readers will notice them?\nDoes it sound like a capable colleague wrote it?",

44	          "body": "re-write this to be as per the following:\n\nThe current shaping pack contains 12 skills (2,599 lines), but it fails its own authoring standards: three skills exceed the 300-line limit, and overlapping instructions force users to guess the correct routing every session\n.\nWe are currently holding five updates (N02–N06) because we have not yet decided how to merge legacy content or where to insert [GATE] headers\n.   "old_string": "## Problem\n\nTwelve skills carry the pack's shaping half today — 2,599 lines at the 2026-06-12 baseline, plus two execution-side survivors. Their When-to-use sections overlap, so every session opens with a routing guess, and `using-this-pack` papers over the ambiguity instead of removing it. Three skills break the pack's own 300-line cap (`delivery-shape` 381, `plan-review` 369, `roadmap-shape` 303): the pack fails the authoring standard it enforces on its own content.\n\nFour door builds (N02–N05) and a utility pass (N06) wait in the cycle to consolidate this, but they block on decisions no single node can make safely alone: which sections of the eight legacy skills fold into which door, what dies deliberately, which files survive, what everything is named, and where every `[GATE]` lands. If each door decides for itself, the four judgement calls drift apart, and a dropped gate surfaces only at the benchmark (N07) or in a live shaping run — never at review. The source library already contains one silent gate loss (delivery-shape, see Context); this doc converts that loss from undetected drift into a tracked decision.\n\nThe cost of staying here lands on named parties. The door authors (N02–N06) cannot write their task breakdowns until this doc exists. The N08 sweep needs one deletion list, not five. Drain-cycle workers and Anton pay the routing and context cost every session. Initiative C's supervisor prompts will cite door verbs literally, so an unstable name breaks them.\n\nToday no absorption map, measured set, naming decision, or gate inventory exists. This doc fixes all four, so the door builds become mechanical compression jobs against a stable spec: each door's Done-when checks `wc -l`, its gate headers against the inventory, and `install.sh` resolution, and the measured set stays ≤2,200 lines.",
  "new_string": "## Problem\n\nThe shaping pack contains twelve skills (2,599 lines at the 2026-06-12 baseline), and it fails its own authoring standards: three skills exceed the 300-line cap (`delivery-shape` 381, `plan-review` 369, `roadmap-shape` 303), and overlapping When-to-use instructions force a routing guess at the start of every session — `using-this-pack` papers over the ambiguity instead of removing it.\n\nWe are holding five updates (N02–N06) because we have not yet decided how to merge the legacy content, what everything is named, or where every `[GATE]` lands. If the door authors make these choices independently, the logic drifts, risking silent gate losses like the one already found in delivery-shape (see Context). The delay prevents the authors from writing their task breakdowns, leaves the N08 sweep without a single deletion list, costs every session a routing guess and extra context, and blocks initiative C, whose supervisor prompts will cite the door verbs literally.\n\nThis document provides the consolidation maps and naming specs required to unblock those updates. We reach the success state (KR1) when the measured set totals 2,200 lines or fewer and every update passes its automated line-count, gate-header, and `install.sh` resolution checks."
}

#### 7f. Add model routing

Apply the 5-axis rubric from `references/task-sizing.md`:

```text
RC, SC, HS, SR, OR
```

Score each axis Low / Med / High, derive the tier and review flag, and append a `Model:` annotation.

The annotation must begin with:

```text
Model:
```

A task without `Model:` is incomplete.

### 8. Emit the file-set

Write the plan using the layout and frontmatter rules in `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md`.

Use numeric prefixes for deterministic order:

```text
D1-<slug>/
N01-<slug>.md
N02-<slug>.md
```

Pad node numbers past nine.

The root `README.md` must include the hand-count manifest the walker reproduces:

```markdown
| Tracker artefact | Source layer | Count |
|------------------|--------------|------:|
| Milestones | deliverables (`D*`) | **<n>** |
| Issues | nodes (`N*`) | **<n>** |
| Sub-issues | tasks (`- [ ]` lines) | **<n>** |
```

### 9. Verify the plan

Run:

```bash
bin/walk-delivery-plan <plan>
```

```

If either fails, fix the emitted file-set. Do not relax the gate.

Then dispatch the `writing-editor` persona (`agents/writing-editor/AGENT.md`) over the emitted node and task bodies, per the dispatch protocol in `docs/adr/0003-persona-contract-and-dispatch-protocol.md` — the `Agent` tool on Claude Code, inline self-review on a non-Claude worker. Apply its line-level rewrites. On `reject`, repair and re-run once; if it still rejects, carry the remaining notes forward as `accept with notes` rather than blocking. The plan is not complete until the verdict is `accept` or `accept with notes`.

## Red flags

- The plan invents outcomes, KRs, acceptance criteria, or design decisions.
- A deliverable serves zero outcome items or more than one outcome item.
- An outcome item has no deliverable.
- A design-doc-worthy deliverable has no first `design-doc` node.
- An accepted design doc input creates a duplicate design-doc node without a new design question.
- A reversible, single-surface deliverable was given an unnecessary design-doc node.
- A node is missing `type`, trace field, or `maps_to`.
- A node omits one of the five required body sections.
- A node inlines a delegated discipline instead of linking to its source skill or reference.
- A setup/scaffolding task appears before the first risk-facing task.
- A skeleton proves mocked plumbing instead of the real risky seam.
- A core-logic-risk story starts with a skeleton that stubs the core risk.
- A task lacks `Done when:` or `Model:`.
- A task has multiple independent completion criteria.
- A cross-team or external dependency is not flagged inline.
- The plan was declared complete without running both gates.

## Exit criteria

The skill is complete when:

- The delivery-plan file-set exists.
- `bin/walk-delivery-plan <plan>` exits 0.
- `bin/check-plan-framing <plan>` exits 0.
- The Step 9 writing-editor pass returned `accept` or `accept with notes`.

## Related

- `initiative-shape` - produces committed initiatives this skill can consume.
- `design-doc` - owns design-doc structure for design-doc-worthy deliverables.
- `writing-refinement` - prose skill node and task bodies are written and reviewed against (`references/plans-okrs.md`, `references/tasks.md`, `style-rules.md`).
- `agents/writing-editor/AGENT.md` - the writing-editor persona dispatched at Step 9.
- `docs/delivery-shape-contract.md` - plan artefact contract, layers, node vocabulary, frontmatter, and cross-reference rules.
- `bin/walk-delivery-plan` - deterministic reader and manifest checker.
- `bin/check-plan-framing` - node/task framing gate.
- `references/task-sizing.md` - 5-axis model-routing rubric.

> AGENT

**Q3 is resolved and recorded.** Three decisions landed in the doc:

1. **The seven-file measured set is confirmed**, router included. The baseline reconciliation is now in the Q3 row too: the project's "11 skills / 2,357 lines" matches the twelve shaping skills minus the router at 2026-06-10 sizes, so excluding the transferred pr-prepare/verify-implementation moves no goalposts — they were never in the denominator — and counting the router makes the KR slightly harder, which you accepted as the honest direction.
2. **2,200 stays the KR number** — a brake, not a target; the per-skill ≤300 cap is what drives compression, and the slack absorbs shape:design's possible flagged 350.
3. **New citation guardrail in Constraints II**: content moved from a counted `SKILL.md` into an uncounted reference file must be cited at the exact workflow step that needs it, and the N03–N05 reviews check that citation exists. This closes the loophole your Q1 answer opened — reference files are free because they load on demand, not because they're a place to hide prose.

One question left: **Q4** — delivery-shape's six de-marked gates (D2–D7). The doc recommends re-marking them as `### N. Gate:` headers at the N02 re-house, on the evidence that the substance survived and the demotion looks like rewrite collateral rather than intent. The alternative is recording the demotion as deliberate. Interview on this one too, or just call it? (Fair warning: the interview here is short — the only real question is whether *you* de-marked them on purpose in the `372f09f` rewrite, and you're the only person who knows.)

> DEVELOPER

when i de-marked them i did it as a part of an editorial change. lets go first principles here. what do the explicit gate headers actually do? what are the gates for? why is this even important? what would happen if these weren't here or more subtle like they are now?

> AGENT

Good question to go first-principles on, because the answer explains why this pack is built the way it is. Let me take it in layers: what the header mechanically is, what it does at three different moments, and what the evidence says happens without it.

## What a gate header actually is

A regular workflow step is an instruction: "do X." A gate is a **stop condition**: "verify X holds; if it doesn't, halt or loop — you may not proceed." The `### N. Gate:` header marks the difference structurally. The anatomy spec and CLAUDE.md both attach mandatory semantics to that exact header form ("the gate semantics are mandatory — do not remove a gate or make it optional"). So the header isn't decoration; it's the syntax that binds the rule.

## The three moments where the marker earns its keep

**1. At runtime — prominence governs compliance.** An LLM executing a 390-line skill is under context pressure, and its native failure mode is momentum: perform each step approximately, keep moving, arrive at an output. A "must" buried mid-paragraph gets glossed; a structural header that says *Gate:* survives skimming and tells the model this step's output is binary and failing means stop, not "do your best and continue." The de-marked gates still *say* the right things — but as prose, they compete for attention with everything else instead of outranking it.

**2. At verification time — only structure is greppable.** This pack's entire bet is deterministic shell checks as fitness functions. "Did the fold keep the gates?" is only a checkable question if gates have a machine-findable signature. Substance-as-prose is invisible to every grep, which is exactly how your 7→1 loss stayed silent: the semantics survived, the *countable surface* vanished, and nothing could notice. The marker is what converts gate preservation from a judgement call into a diff.

**3. At edit time — the marker is a "load-bearing, do not compress" flag.** This is the immediate, practical one: **N02 is about to compress this exact file by ~90 lines.** A density pass legitimately deletes anything that looks like explanatory prose. Six of your seven gates currently look like explanatory prose. Without re-marking, the compression that Q1 just authorized is the likeliest mechanism by which the substance finally dies — politely, defensibly, in review.

## What actually happens when they're subtle — by gate

The risk isn't uniform across D2–D7, and this is worth seeing clearly:

- **Mechanically backed gates (D4 five-section body, D5 `Done when:` on every task):** if the model glosses them, `walk-delivery-plan`/`check-plan-framing` catch the violation at step 9. The marker here is belt-and-suspenders — it saves a fix loop, not the contract.
- **Pure-judgement gates (D1 committed-input, D3 design-first, D6 size judgement):** nothing mechanical backstops these. If the model glosses D3, a design-worthy deliverable gets no design-doc node and a build starts on a misunderstood problem — the exact failure the skill exists to prevent, and no script will ever flag it. These live or die entirely on prominence in context.
- **D7 verify-plan is the meta-gate:** it's the step that *invokes* the checkers. If it gets skipped, the mechanical safety net never fires at all. Of the six, this is the one a marker protects most.

And the evidence that erosion is real isn't hypothetical: the 7→1 drift happened **in two days, in your own repo, with you editing attentively** — you just told me you didn't even intend it. That's the strongest possible argument that prose-only mandatory semantics don't survive contact with normal editing.

## The honest counterweight

Gate inflation is a real failure in the other direction — if every step is a gate, the marker stops outranking anything and you're back to prose. Seven gates in the pack's core emitter is a deliberate density, not inflation; but it's worth keeping the bar where the anatomy puts it (a gate is a checkpoint whose failure must halt the run, nothing less).

Given that you've confirmed the demotion was editorial collateral — which is precisely the hypothesis the doc recorded — Q4 has a natural answer:

1	# ADR 0004 — Execution verb namespace (`exec:*`)
2	
3	- **Status:** Accepted
4	- **Date:** 2026-06-10
5	- **Serves:** Initiative D2 / KR1 — drained issues reach Done with a merged PR and full review trail (N04–N08).
6	- **Premise for:** Initiative B (verb consolidation consumes this namespace table) and Initiative C (the drain-cycle supervisor binds the front-door verb literally).
7	- **Record of:** the namespace decision in `docs/design-docs/execution-workflow/design-doc.md` (N04). The design doc carries the full alternatives analysis and skill graph; this ADR is the durable decision downstream skills bind to.
8	
9	## Context
10	
11	D2 authors five execution-side skills (front door, build, debug/simplify, review fan-out, verify, PR finishing). Each must be reachable by name from the front-door skill, and the drain-cycle supervisor in initiative C will name the front-door verb literally in `drain_cycle/prompt.py`. The verb namespace is a one-way door: once the supervisor prompt and workers' muscle memory bind, a rename costs a coordinated migration across every drainer.
12	
13	Shaping verbs (`shape:*`) are already established. The execution half was unnamed. Four candidates were weighed in the N04 design doc:
14	
15	1. **Bare verbs** (`/pickup`, `/build`, `/review`) — rejected: `/review` collides with installed packs (`code-review`, `crit`, built-in `review`); no slash-command grouping.
16	2. **`exec:*` prefix** — mirrors `shape:*`; two clean halves of one namespace; per-verb rename stays local to the prefix.
17	3. **`drain:*` prefix** — rejected: couples the namespace to one supervisor, re-creating the pack-vs-supervisor coupling initiative C exists to remove.
18	4. **Nameless** — rejected: no collision detection, nothing for initiative B to consolidate to.
19	
20	**Supervisor-binding audit (performed before pinning).** A read of current `drain_cycle/prompt.py` confirmed the migration surface is bounded: the supervisor emits `/code-review-and-quality` (4 string locations) and `/shape:task` (verify-flow directive only); the rest of the completion sequence is inlined prose. Adopting `exec:*` is a bounded string swap plus an additive replacement, not a coordinated cross-drainer migration. The audit also corrected a stale assumption — `/shape:verify-implementation`, `/shape:pr-prepare`, and `/shape:pr-respond` are not in current source.
21	
22	## Decision
23	
24	**Adopt the `exec:*` prefix for every execution-side verb.** Reserved namespace table (both halves, single source of truth for initiative B):
25	
26	| Half | Prefix | Verbs (this pack) |
27	|---|---|---|
28	| Shaping | `shape:` | `shape:idea-triage`, `shape:initiative`, `shape:roadmap`, `shape:delivery`, `shape:design-doc`, `shape:plan-review`, … (existing) |
29	| Execution | `exec:` | `exec:pickup` (front door), `exec:breakdown`, `exec:build`, `exec:debug`, `exec:simplify`, `exec:review`, `exec:verify`, `exec:finish` |
30	
31	`exec:review` is N03's already-shipped `execution-review` skill, renamed under the prefix at N05 authoring time.
32	
33	## Consequences
34	
35	**Positive**
36	- Mirrors `shape:*`; one consistent namespacing rule across the whole pack.
37	- Per-verb renames stay local to the prefix — low reversal cost for a single verb.
38	- Initiative B consolidates against one reserved table; initiative C binds one front-door verb.
39	
40	**Negative / costs**
41	- Verbose at the slash-command surface versus bare verbs. Accepted — discovery grouping and collision-avoidance outweigh verbosity.
42	- The drain-cycle cutover requires a bounded edit to `prompt.py` (the N10 pointer-swap). Scheduled, not free.
43	
44	## Scope
45	
46	This ADR pins the prefix and reserves the leaf names. The skill graph, handoff contract, and per-skill behaviour live in the N04 design doc; the skills themselves are authored in N05–N08. Any change to the prefix or a reserved leaf name requires a follow-up ADR.
47	
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/anton/src/agent-skills-shaper/docs/adr/0005-shaping-door-leaf-names.md",
  "content": "# ADR 0005 — Shaping door leaf names (`shape:idea` / `shape:project` / `shape:design` / `shape:delivery`)\n\n- **Status:** Accepted\n- **Date:** 2026-06-12\n- **Serves:** Initiative B / KR1 — the shaping library consolidates to four phase doors plus utilities, measured set ≤2,200 lines (N01–N08).\n- **Premise for:** N02–N06 (the door builds and utility pass bind these directory, wrapper, and verb names) and Initiative C (supervisor prompts cite the door verbs literally).\n- **Record of:** the naming decision in `docs/designs/front-door-ia.md` (N01). The IA doc carries the full alternatives analysis, absorption map, survivor manifest, and gate inventory; this ADR is the durable decision downstream skills bind to. ADR 0004 remains the record of the `shape:`/`exec:` prefix split.\n\n## Context\n\nADR 0004 reserved both halves of the verb namespace and left the shaping-half leaf names to initiative B. The names are a one-way door: `install.sh` publishes them into every install through wrapper generation and `shape-*` symlinks, `using-this-pack` and README cite them, and initiative C's supervisor prompts will bind them literally. Four alternatives were weighed in the IA doc:\n\n1. **Bare phase-leaf directories** (`skills/idea/`, `skills/project/`, `skills/design/`, `skills/delivery/`) — wrapper leaf = directory leaf = frontmatter `name`; `install.sh` derives the rest. The pack already applies this rule (`delivery.md` → `/shape:delivery`).\n2. **Suffixed directories** (`skills/idea-shape/`) — rejected: installed names stutter (`shape-idea-shape`) and the naming rule degrades from a convention to a lookup table.\n3. **Keep current directories, retarget wrappers only** — rejected: permanent verb/directory drift; the zero-legacy-citations check can never pass.\n4. **Do nothing** — rejected: KR1 fails by definition.\n\n## Decision\n\n**Adopt alternative 1.** The reserved shaping leaves:\n\n| Verb | Directory | Wrapper | Installed symlink |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `shape:idea` | `skills/idea/` | `.claude/commands/idea.md` | `~/.claude/skills/shape-idea` |\n| `shape:project` | `skills/project/` | `.claude/commands/project.md` | `~/.claude/skills/shape-project` |\n| `shape:design` | `skills/design/` | `.claude/commands/design.md` | `~/.claude/skills/shape-design` |\n| `shape:delivery` | `skills/delivery/` | `.claude/commands/delivery.md` | `~/.claude/skills/shape-delivery` |\n| `shape:plan-review` | `skills/plan-review/` (unchanged) | unchanged | `~/.claude/skills/shape-plan-review` |\n| `shape:render-html` | `skills/render-html/` (unchanged) | unchanged | `~/.claude/skills/shape-render-html` |\n\nOne convention encodes the table: wrapper leaf = directory leaf = frontmatter `name`; `install.sh` derives the symlink (`shape-<leaf>`) and prunes stale links on re-install. The help text at `install.sh:161–173` is hand-owned and updated per door node.\n\n## Consequences\n\n**Positive**\n- The naming rule stays a convention, not a lookup table; `install.sh` needs no per-skill cases.\n- Leaf names never appear without the `shape-`/`shape:` prefix on any installed surface, so generic leaves (\"design\") stay unambiguous.\n\n**Negative / costs**\n- A future rename is a coordinated sweep across README, router, help text, and initiative C prompts. Accepted — this is why the IA doc, not a door node, decided the names.\n- ADR 0004's verb column listed the pre-consolidation shaping verbs (`shape:idea-triage`, `shape:initiative`, …); this ADR supersedes that column's shaping entries. The `exec:*` half is untouched.\n\n## Scope\n\nThis ADR pins the four door leaves and confirms the three utility leaves. The absorption map, measured set, frontmatter cut, and gate inventory live in `docs/designs/front-door-ia.md`. Any change to a leaf name requires a follow-up ADR.\n"
}
```
